ParlaMint-AT_2000-03-02-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00013_d7e2488	Federal Chancellor, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-03-02-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00013_d7e2504	Mr Bösch! You know that in our government programme we have agreed to give the greatest attention to good cooperation between the federal government and countries. I very much hope that the long-term wishes and concerns expressed by the federal states will be better fulfilled in a comprehensive throw of improvement, which actually takes account of the state idea of Austria. I think that the following urgent measures are urgent: firstly, the establishment of a national administrative jurisdiction. . I have already mentioned that, in particular, with regard to Article 6 of the European Convention on Human Rights, matters to be attributed to administrative law under our national administrative system are so-called civil rights claims. To that end, we need an administrative body, a jurisdiction of the first instance, because only it guarantees that the requirements of the Convention are met. This also significantly reduces the nuisance that now exists in the courts of public law. There is almost no legal refusal when you have many years of waiting. . I believe that this will also ensure improved legal protection for individuals. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] In addition, in a comprehensive novel to the Federal Constitutional Act, we want the so-called country model, the federal states of the 20th century. ==References====External links==* Official website In doing so, the national administrative courts are to be given the possibility of a fundamental cassatory decision. The second major issue, which is particularly important for the economy, for the location of Austria and thus for jobs, is a modern plant law. We want to continue the way to a uniform investment law. The first stage – to be achieved in the first 100 days of this government – is to implement the relevant EU Directives. The second step is to implement a federal unit plant law. The third point is the absence of the indirect federal administration. We confess ourselves to the preparation of a comprehensive federal constitution law in which the absence of the indirect federal administration is to be anchored. In principle, the matters carried out in central federal administration are to be dealt with in Article 11. In addition, Article 10 of the Treaty also provides for the transfer of previous cases in Article 11. With the exception of plant law – I would like to point out – the materials that the second-third majority need, and I am already inviting all the political groups here in the House to enter into appropriate political discussions so that these important countries can be implemented as quickly as possible.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-03-02-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00013_d7e3452	Please, Mr. Federal Chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-03-02-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00013_d7e3468	In the next few weeks, we want to propose to the House a programme, a set of measures by means of applications to which housing and rental costs are significantly reduced. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] The second measure will be a standardisation of the settlement by means of ÖNORM. This will create transparency and reduce housing costs. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] As far as tax law is concerned, we shall examine whether a continuation of tax exemption is possible for half the rental interest reserves. With this, we could hold back the feared §-18 procedures. In addition, we want to intensify the formation of property. It is our concern, and it is also a quite social-political objective to pursue housing policy in the sense of a wide range of property formation. We will also propose appropriate legal measures to ensure that tenants have the option to acquire property later on.

And if you look closely at that, you can see: Not in Burgenland and not in Lower Austria, but where the agricultural area is the smallest, is the forest part of the farmers – thank God! – the biggest. For example, it lies in Tyrol at 20 hectares, in Carinthia at 18 hectares. I admit that the forests there are not very well maintained, but so far you have had no chance to do so because it costs more than it brings. We must act on this because I believe that after Agenda 2000, it is a good opportunity for our small and medium-sized farms to use the forest. They not only live in addition to the forest, but they also produce something – unconscious or conscious – that is, an increase in the quality of the wood. Our chance in the wood market, in Europe – with the Finns, also in Germany, in Slovakia – and worldwide, is not in the forts, our chance lies in the quality! ParlaMint-AT_2000-03-02-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00013_d7e19609	Mr President. High house! Mr. Federal Minister! The water protection report before us contains a large number of very striking statements about our Austrian water protection policy. But let me go back to the brink of the failure of recent years. Ladies and gentlemen! Austria is about to be sued before the European Court of Justice for breach of the Water Protection Directive. According to the Commission opinion pursuant to Article 226 of the EU Treaty of 21. In January 2000, Austria infringes the Nitrates Directive because it does not impose binding measures, such as time and surface restrictions on fertilisers and sufficient dungeon tanks in Austria for nitrate-loaded groundwater areas. Ladies and gentlemen! This is a failure of the last ten years. Since 1990, since the Novelle Water Law Act, these implementing provisions have expired. Mr Molterer, Minister of State! You too have already been in charge of this area, and you have been a good man, Mr Minister. You know that on the basis of understandable conflicts of interest and conflicts between the claims of intensive production areas of agriculture on the one hand and the water protection regulations and water protection issues on the other. How will that be in the future? – Become an Environment Minister They are also responsible for environmental, waste management and conservation issues. You also have a wealth of conflicts of interest which we believe you cannot handle in this constellation. The European Commission is meeting Austria at a wound point because the measures envisaged in the Novelle Law on Water Law in 1990 have not been taken to reduce nitrate pollution of groundwater. No head of state in Austria has adopted a regulation imposing economic restrictions on farmers in groundwater-hazardous areas, although in Austria at least 150 water supply plants exceed the drinking water limit values – ladies and gentlemen – is a signal! – and in these cases a so-called suspension decision had to be issued. Only in the agricultural environment programmes – that is right, Mr Federal Minister – are the measures for groundwater protection provided, but these measures are voluntary – you know that, the current water protection report makes this clear – and that is, I believe, crucial and important – de facto not effective, Mr Federal Minister! Long-term trend studies have shown that 23.3 percent of all observation values had a deterioration of the nitrate value at 934 of these measuring points, while only 10 percent of the measuring points had an improvement. The rest remained at the same level. In your water protection report, Mr. Federal Minister, 48 percent of the total area is designated as a nitrate-hazardous area – 48 percent of an area of 1.2 million hectares. That is, some 660 000 hectares in Austria are declared as endangered nitrate regions. The Water Protection Report states very clearly: The causes of the extremely laborious progress in taking legislative measures to improve the quality of the groundwaters affected are essentially – please listen carefully! – in conflicts of interest in establishing location-related management restrictions, in securing compensation payments and finally in the matter of a coordinated Procedure for granting grants under § 33 of the Water Law Act and the possibility of public support. That's exactly the conflict, Minister of State! We have conflicts of interest at many levels, including at the level of support. Mr. Federal Minister! What has happened to those recommendations that we can read in your Green Report 1998, namely those of the § 7 Commission on reducing groundwater pollution. It states that the number of thresholds exceeded for nitrates increased by 2% between 1995 and 1997. That's the big problem. Here we have an increase in nitrate loads. This §-7 Commission calls on you, for example, to introduce a stockpiling programme under the Austrian environmental programme – a stockpiling of animal stocks that are currently causing these nitrate infections and these nitrate loads. Finally, let me address a very central area of waste water disposal, especially in rural areas. The lack of harmonisation between the Environmental Promotion Act and the Water Economic Act is still on the list of unfinished acts. Over the next ten years, more than 200 billion schillings of investment will take place in this area. It is not provided by law that variant comparisons are made mandatory, that comparisons must be made which individual variants can, as it were, be preferred, namely variants which are more ecological, which are decentralised and which also provide small regional solutions. Let me summarize, ladies and gentlemen: This water protection report shows in a dramatic way that the measures taken so far at the preventive level, for example by means of the environmental programme, as well as at the legal level, are extremely inadequate, even to a great extent characterized by toughness, low target accuracy and too much ease. We shall vote against this report, ladies and gentlemen, because it contains inconsistencies, because it makes statements that contradict each other. [[Abg. Dr. Pumberger: What does "inconsistencies" mean?]] Among other things, this report also states: With the use of traditional groundwater protection instruments, it has been possible to avoid at least another increase in the levels of nitrate in groundwater in recent years. - Quote. This is contained in a report which, at the same time, claims the opposite in many other places. Such a report is inconsistent. Federal Minister! We urge you to make it clear.
